Critical flavors: Production 100/Science 100/Spaceship 100 for rapid wonder and parts construction. Gold 90 for Aluminum acquisition via trade with Songhai (29 stockpile) or China peace deal (14 stockpile). Defense 95/Antiair 90/Nuke 95/UseNuke 80 against Egyptian XCOM encirclement (22,000 vs 2,183 military, 10:1 ratio). If Egypt completes spaceship first (33% progress, 2 parts done), game over. This is final sprint for survival.

  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Enrico Dandolo, leader of Venice (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Venice
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Merchant of Venice, Replacing Great Merchant (Requires technology: None)
        - Unique Unit: Great Galleass, Replacing Galleass (Requires technology: Gunpowder)
        - Unique Building: Piazza San Marco, Replacing National Monument (Requires technology: Writing)
        - Civilization Ability: Cannot create Settlers or annex Cities. Trade Route cap doubled, target restrictions removed. Free Merchant of Venice at Trade. Puppets have -30% Yield penalties, can use Gold and gain Happiness like normal Cities.
      - Leader: Enrico Dandolo
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      - Stealth: Allows you to build the Stealth Bomber, the most powerful Ranged Unit in the game.
        Completing it would unlock: Globalization, Nuclear Fusion
      
      ## Policies
      - Covert Action (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Covert Action: Receive 100 Spy Points. When successfully rigging an election in a City-State, the Influence you gain and the Influence other Players lose are increased by +50%. Spies stationed in a foreign City gain +10 Network Points per turn.
      - Universal Suffrage (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Universal Suffrage: A Golden Age begins, and Golden Ages last 50% longer. Cities produce +1 Happiness.
      - Creative Expression (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Creative Expression: +2 Tourism from Great Works. Museums, Broadcast Towers, Opera Houses, and Amphitheaters gain +3 Golden Age Points and Culture.
      - Draft Registration (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Draft Registration: Military Units purchased with Gold receive full XP.
      - Civil Society (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Civil Society: Specialists consume 2 Food less than normal (minimum 1 Food). Farms, Plantations, Camps, and all Unique Improvements produce +4 Food.
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Rights of Man (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Villages.
        - * +10% Science during Golden Ages.
      - Justice (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - * Cities with a garrison gain +25% Ranged Combat Strength.
        - * Royal Guardhouse built in Capital (+3 Production, +2 Defense, +50 Hit Points, +10% Military Supply from Population in all Cities, 1 Engineer Slot).
      - Sovereignty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Culture cost of tiles reduced by 20% (exponentially) in all Cities.
        - * Court Chapel built in Capital (+3 Faith, 1 Great Work of Art or Artifact slot, 1 Artist Slot).
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Mercantilism (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Science and Culture from Markets, Caravansaries, Customs Houses, Banks, and Stock Exchanges.
        - * Cities earn +10 Science when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona

        - Rationale: EGYPTIAN XCOM ENCIRCLEMENT: Egypt has deployed Mechanized Infantry, 3 XCOM Squads, Mobile SAM, Fighter, and Rocket Missile to puppet Nara zone directly adjacent to our core territory. This is existential threat level. Zero changes to persona - absolute submission maintained at 100/100 relations. Wonder competitiveness at 5 maintained for potential Hubble/NASA wonder race. Any provocation = immediate destruction given 10:1 military ratio (19,572 vs 1,925).
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: None
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Rights of Man (Policy)
        - Rationale: RATIONALISM COMPLETION CRITICAL: Rights of Man (-1 Unhappiness from all Needs, +2 Production/Gold from Villages, +10% Science during Golden Ages) completes Rationalism policy branch. With 5-city puppet empire, the unhappiness reduction provides critical infrastructure efficiency. The +10% Science multiplier is essential to close technology gap with Egypt (81 techs vs our 69) and race for spaceship victory before Egypt completes their 3rd part (currently at 33%). Freedom tenets can wait - infrastructure efficiency and science acceleration paramount with potentially only ~30-40 turns remaining before either Egyptian science victory or Indian diplomatic victory (88%, vote in 3 turns).
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Bunkeya
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 95
        - Rationale: Bunkeya: CRITICAL - Lost alliance to Songhai (211 vs 106) despite our spy rigging elections. Quest to spy on Gandhi for 201 influence would bring us to 307 vs Songhai's 211, instantly reclaiming this critical 4th ally before World Congress. We discovered Venice sent Envoy (ID 6968) toward this area - presumably to complete spy quest. Maintain max priority.
        - UpdatedTurn: 224
        
        ### Riga
        - Public: 80
        - Private: 70
        - Rationale: Riga: Newly discovered, allied with India (135 influence). With 2 turns remaining on religious quest, low chance to win. Maintain friendly stance for potential future opportunities - India's ally but distant from our core interests.
        - UpdatedTurn: 227
        
        ### Raqmu
        - Public: 50
        - Private: 40
        - Rationale: Raqmu: Newly discovered, Aztec ally (225). Standard priority - Aztecs not currently hostile. Establish presence with gold investment if excess resources available after higher priority city-states.
        - UpdatedTurn: 247
        
        ### Kwa-Bulawayo
        - Public: -80
        - Private: -90
        - Rationale: Kwa-Bulawayo: HOSTILE. Lost alliance due to war with China - dropped from 251 influence to -60. China's ally declared war on Venice when I attacked China. This city-state is now hostile and contributes to my diplomatic victory setback. Mark as low priority hostile target for potential peace negotiations recovery.
        - UpdatedTurn: 257
        
        ### Zuunmod
        - Public: -80
        - Private: -90
        - Rationale: Zuunmod: HOSTILE. Lost alliance due to war with China - dropped from 195 influence to -60. China's ally declared war on Venice. Despite previous spy operations to flip this city-state, the war declaration reversed all progress. Now hostile and must be recovered through peace if possible.
        - UpdatedTurn: 257
        
        ### Jerusalem
        - Public: -80
        - Private: -90
        - Rationale: Jerusalem: HOSTILE. Lost potential alliance due to war with China - dropped from 182 influence to -60. This was my TOP PRIORITY target to flip from China, but war declaration made it hostile instead. The Great Admiral quest would have given me this delegate, but now it's unreachable until peace. Critical loss for diplomatic victory.
        - UpdatedTurn: 257
        
        ### Sokoto
        - Public: -70
        - Private: -80
        - Rationale: Sokoto: HOSTILE. Lost potential alliance due to war with China. Was planning to target with gold investment (387 GPT available), but now at war and unreachable. China's ally cut off this potential 12th+ delegate. Must recover through peace if diplomatic victory remains possible.
        - UpdatedTurn: 257
        
        ### Valletta
        - Public: -30
        - Private: -50
        - Rationale: Valletta lost to Aztec influence (dropped to -60). No longer viable target with 14 turns remaining. Abandoning to focus resources on defensible allies (Abernethy, Samarkand) and science infrastructure.
        - UpdatedTurn: 269
        
        ### Abernethy
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 95
        - Rationale: Abernethy: Maximum priority defense. At 427 influence but Egypt competing aggressively at 314. This coastal city-state provides critical delegate and trade route slot. Spy Ercole should continue defensive rigging. Cannot afford to lose this ally - it's one of my few remaining secure delegates with diplomatic victory abandoned.
        - UpdatedTurn: 271
        
        ### La Venta
        - Public: 90
        - Private: 85
        - Rationale: La Venta: High priority maintenance. At 529 influence with spy quest completing soon (+264 influence from spy mission vs China), this delegate is secure. Located far from threats, provides valuable trade route and delegate for World Congress science resolutions. Maintain alliance but don't over-invest gold with diplomatic victory abandoned.
        - UpdatedTurn: 271
        
        ### Oslo
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 95
        - Rationale: Oslo: Maximum priority maintenance. Ally at 251 influence with sphere of influence enacted (permanent ally unless repealed). This is a secure delegate that cannot be lost to normal influence competition. Maya has 69 influence and is nearby - they could theoretically attack the city-state directly when our peace treaty expires. Must ensure military protection for this critical ally.
        - UpdatedTurn: 330
        
        ### Salem
        - Public: 50
        - Private: 40
        - Rationale: Lost Salem alliance to Egypt (147 vs our 135 influence, down from 251). Egypt proposing Sphere of Influence on Salem which will make them permanent ally. Acknowledging reality by reducing priority from 100/95 to 50/40 - still cordial but no longer viable target for competition given Egypt's 9357 military and 583 influence. Focus resources on secure allies Oslo (sphere, 539 influence) and Kwa-Bulawayo (ally, 371 influence) for upcoming World Congress in 4 turns.
        - UpdatedTurn: 341
        
        ### Samarkand
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 95
        - Rationale: Samarkand: Just secured alliance (486 influence). This delegate is now one of our most secure. Egypt competing aggressively at 354 vs our 486. Spy Filomena should rig defensively. Cannot afford to lose this delegate.
        - UpdatedTurn: 349
        
        ### India
        - Public: 90
        - Private: 85
        - Rationale: INDIA DIPLOMATIC COMPETITOR MANAGEMENT: India at 76% diplomatic victory (33/43 delegates) with Freedom ideology alignment. Despite being our top UN rival, they pose no military threat and share our ideology. High friendship prevents them using 33 delegates against our interests while we pursue Science Victory. Stable relationship necessary to avoid opening another front.
        - UpdatedTurn: 387
        
        ### The Aztecs
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: AZTEC TRIVIAL WAR NORMALIZATION: War with Aztecs has only 4% score with minimal weariness. They fight Songhai/India and pose zero threat to Venice. 0/0 relationship allows AI to negotiate peace, freeing our minimal military resources for defense against Egyptian encirclement. No strategic value in maintaining distant hostilities.
        - UpdatedTurn: 407
        
        ### Japan
        - Public: 90
        - Private: 85
        - Rationale: JAPAN VASSAL NORMALIZATION: Tokyo liberation complete - Japan restored as civilization and accepted as voluntary vassal (confirmed by DoF, shared religion Shinto, embassy). Updating from neutral 0/0 (Turn 373) to 90/85 reflects master-vassal alliance reality. Secures northeastern flank while Egyptian XCOMs operate in Nara/Tokyo zones.
        - UpdatedTurn: 411
        
        ### The Maya
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 95
        - Rationale: Maya relationship maintained at maximum despite ideological differences (Freedom vs Autocracy). Their 4,344 military with Close proximity creates existential two-front vulnerability if combined with Egyptian XCOMs. Peace treaty expired turn 377 - we're in danger window. Cannot afford any military distraction from Apollo Program construction in 8 turns. Maximum relationship prevents backstab while we race for Science Victory.
        - UpdatedTurn: 417
        
        ### Egypt
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: EGYPTIAN XCOM ENCIRCLEMENT - ABSOLUTE SUBMISSION: Egyptian Mechanized Infantry, XCOM Squads, Mobile SAM, Fighter, Rocket Missile operating in Nara zone (13245) directly adjacent to Venice/Cattaro core. 19,601 vs 2,203 military strength = 9:1 annihilation ratio. Perfect 100/100 relations maintained - any friction = immediate destruction. They paid 85 GPT for our Uranium indicating mutual nuclear interest. Survival depends on complete invisibility and submission until nuclear deterrent operational or spaceship launched.
        - UpdatedTurn: 420
        
        ### China
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: CHINA PEACE FOR ALUMINUM - EXISTENTIAL PRIORITY: China possesses 14 Aluminum - we have 0 and require 4+ for spaceship parts construction. Currently at war (44% war score in our favor, Beijing at 92% health under Songhai pressure). Maximum 100/100 relationship signals absolute willingness to negotiate peace immediately. Peace required to: 1) Trade for Aluminum (existential blocker for spaceship), 2) Restore trade routes (+150+ GPT revenue), 3) End war weariness affecting happiness and production, 4) Focus all resources on Apollo Program construction and spaceship race against Egypt (33% progress). Cannot afford to continue war while facing Egyptian technological superiority and Indian diplomatic victory threat.
        - UpdatedTurn: 421
        
        ### Songhai
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: SONGHAI ALUMINUM ACQUISITION CRITICAL: Songhai is our Defensive Pact ally with 8,494 military strength (only credible deterrent against Egypt's 19,601) and possesses 29 Aluminum stockpile. Current trade deal provides only 1 Aluminum despite our existential need for 4+ to build spaceship parts. Maintaining maximum 100/100 relationship preserves alliance while signaling urgent need for Aluminum renegotiation. With China war blocking their Aluminum trade, Songhai represents our only viable path to spaceship construction materials. Defensive Pact essential for survival against Egyptian XCOM encirclement - cannot risk any relationship deterioration with sole military protector.
        - UpdatedTurn: 421
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: SATELLITES COMPLETED - APOLLO RUSH CRITICAL: Turn 420 completed Satellites technology. Great Engineer (ID 18338) must rush Apollo Program immediately to begin spaceship construction. Current flavors optimized for existential crisis: Production 100/Science 100/Spaceship 100 for rapid wonder and parts construction; Gold 95 for Aluminum acquisition via trade; Defense 90/Nuke 95 against Egyptian XCOM encirclement (19,601 vs 2,182 military ratio). Egypt at 33% spaceship progress (2 parts complete) vs our 0%. India at 88% diplomatic victory (39/43 delegates) with vote in 3 turns. Zero Aluminum available (need 4+ for spaceship parts) - must acquire via Songhai trade (29 Aluminum stockpile, Defensive Pact ally) or China peace deal (14 Aluminum, currently at war). Maintaining absolute submission to Egypt (100/100 relations) remains existential priority - their XCOMs and Mechanized Infantry in adjacent Nara zone (13245) create 9:1 annihilation threat. No flavor adjustments warranted during this critical transition - maximum production and science output essential.
        - GrandStrategy: Spaceship

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 44
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 2 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Indian Sphere of Influence: Riga:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 418
          - Cultural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each World Wonder provides +2 Culture to its city, and each Great Work provides +1 Science to its city.
            - EnactedOn: 418
          - Egyptian Sphere of Influence: Raqmu:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 418
          - World Science Initiative:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians.
              Civilizations with Scores from Technologies and Future Tech below the global median will receive a boost to Food and Science up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: 418
          - Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: 418
          - Historical Landmarks:
            - Description: Great Person tile improvements provide +1 to Food, Production, and Gold when worked, and Landmarks built by Archaeologists provide +1 Science, Culture, and Faith when worked.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Archaeology.
            - EnactedOn: 418
          - Indian Sphere of Influence: Sokoto:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 418
          - World Ideology: Freedom:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: 418
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: 418
          - Indian Sphere of Influence: Dorestad:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 418
          - World Religion: Shinto:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.
            - EnactedOn: 418
          - Casus Belli:
            - Description: The world recognizes the need for justifiable war to resolve diplomatic disputes.
              While this resolution is active, unit maintenance costs are lowered by 15% Gold. 0.5x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 2x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: 418
          - Venetian Sphere of Influence: Oslo:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 418
          - Sanction: The Aztecs:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: 418
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Global Hegemony:
            - Description: Designate a player that could achieve Global Hegemony. If a Civilization receives support from enough Delegates in the Congress, that leader will attain Diplomatic Victory. The number of Delegates needed depends on the number of Civilizations and City-States in the game, and can be seen on the World Congress screen.
              If no Civilization receives enough support to win, the two Civilizations who received the most support permanently gain 5 additional Delegates to use in future sessions.
              May not be proposed by a Civilization. Automatically proposed at regular intervals once a World Ideology Resolution is active.
            - Proposer: Unknown

- replay:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Enrico Dandolo, leader of Venice (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Venice
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Merchant of Venice, Replacing Great Merchant (Requires technology: None)
        - Unique Unit: Great Galleass, Replacing Galleass (Requires technology: Gunpowder)
        - Unique Building: Piazza San Marco, Replacing National Monument (Requires technology: Writing)
        - Civilization Ability: Cannot create Settlers or annex Cities. Trade Route cap doubled, target restrictions removed. Free Merchant of Venice at Trade. Puppets have -30% Yield penalties, can use Gold and gain Happiness like normal Cities.
      - Leader: Enrico Dandolo
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      - Stealth: Allows you to build the Stealth Bomber, the most powerful Ranged Unit in the game.
        Completing it would unlock: Globalization, Nuclear Fusion
      
      ## Policies
      - Covert Action (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Covert Action: Receive 100 Spy Points. When successfully rigging an election in a City-State, the Influence you gain and the Influence other Players lose are increased by +50%. Spies stationed in a foreign City gain +10 Network Points per turn.
      - Universal Suffrage (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Universal Suffrage: A Golden Age begins, and Golden Ages last 50% longer. Cities produce +1 Happiness.
      - Creative Expression (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Creative Expression: +2 Tourism from Great Works. Museums, Broadcast Towers, Opera Houses, and Amphitheaters gain +3 Golden Age Points and Culture.
      - Draft Registration (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Draft Registration: Military Units purchased with Gold receive full XP.
      - Civil Society (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Civil Society: Specialists consume 2 Food less than normal (minimum 1 Food). Farms, Plantations, Camps, and all Unique Improvements produce +4 Food.
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Rights of Man (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Villages.
        - * +10% Science during Golden Ages.
      - Justice (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - * Cities with a garrison gain +25% Ranged Combat Strength.
        - * Royal Guardhouse built in Capital (+3 Production, +2 Defense, +50 Hit Points, +10% Military Supply from Population in all Cities, 1 Engineer Slot).
      - Sovereignty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Culture cost of tiles reduced by 20% (exponentially) in all Cities.
        - * Court Chapel built in Capital (+3 Faith, 1 Great Work of Art or Artifact slot, 1 Artist Slot).
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Mercantilism (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Science and Culture from Markets, Caravansaries, Customs Houses, Banks, and Stock Exchanges.
        - * Cities earn +10 Science when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Exploring Venice's Unique Mechanics**
            
            I'm currently focused on understanding Venice's core mechanics. My thoughts are grappling with how to best leverage their doubled trade route capacity while compensating for the inability to produce settlers or annex cities. This requires a strategic shift towards diplomatic and economic dominance, rather than territorial expansion.
            
            **Analyzing Victory Conditions**
            
            I'm currently analyzing the victory conditions. Egypt is dangerously close to a cultural victory, having achieved influence over all seven civs. Meanwhile, I'm assessing my science progress and the implications of Venice's unique mechanics on the game's trajectory.
            
            **Prioritizing Diplomatic Victory**
            
            I'm re-evaluating my strategy to counter India's imminent diplomatic victory. With only two turns until the vote and India holding 39 delegates, I need to shift my focus immediately. My current economic state, with negative gold per turn, is a significant concern and will require swift adjustments to ensure I can influence the World Congress.
            
            **Choosing Next Technology**
            
            I am evaluating our technology path. With Lasers researched, I'm now weighing Electronics versus Stealth. I'm also looking ahead at available policies, particularly "Rights of Man" and the broader Freedom and Continuing Branch options, to see how they align with my strategic goals.
            
            **Securing Diplomatic Supremacy**
            
            I'm now focused on blocking India's impending diplomatic victory. My thoughts are on how to leverage our trade routes to quickly improve our gold situation to fund necessary actions, while also considering the looming threat of Egypt's cultural and scientific progress.
            
            **Shifting to Defense**
            
            My immediate focus is on survival and blocking immediate victory conditions. I need to analyze if switching our Grand Strategy to United Nations is viable to counter India's diplomatic win, or if maintaining our Space Race focus is more prudent given Egypt's progress. My gold situation requires urgent attention to fund any potential diplomatic maneuvers.
            
            **Boosting Minor Civ Influence**
            
            My focus is now on critically adjusting my persona, specifically increasing MinorCivCompetitiveness. With "Global Hegemony" on the horizon and India so close to victory, my current low score of 1 is a critical vulnerability. I need to secure more influence with city-states to counter their delegate advantage.
            
            **Optimizing Diplomatic Strategy**
            
            I'm now reviewing persona parameters, specifically elevating `MinorCivCompetitiveness` to 10 to counter India's delegate advantage. I'm also evaluating `WonderCompetitiveness` and `DiplomaticBalance`, and considering policies like "Free Trade" or "Mercantilism" to boost our trade income, or potentially Freedom tenets for long-term strategic alignment.
            
            **Selecting Next Policy**
            
            I am prioritizing `Free Trade` for its excellent synergy with Venice's Great Merchants and trade routes, aiming to boost our gold income. Alternatively, a Freedom Tenet like `Civil Society` to enhance specialist output is being considered for growth.
            
            **Adopting Policy for Synergy**
            
            I'm now prioritizing `Free Trade` for its excellent synergy with Venice's Great Merchants and trade routes, aiming to boost our gold income. Alternatively, a Freedom Tenet like `Civil Society` to enhance specialist output is being considered for growth.
            
            **Adjusting City-State Dynamics**
            
            I'm now focused on influencing city-states to counter India's imminent diplomatic victory. My thoughts are on increasing competitiveness and friendliness towards them, leveraging our gold and diplomats to secure their support. This is crucial to deny India the delegates needed for victory, while carefully managing our own diplomatic relationships.
            
            **Boosting Diplomatic Flavor**
            
            My thoughts are now focused on optimizing flavors to counter India's city-state dominance. I'm raising `Diplomacy` to 95 to incentivize the AI to produce diplomats and counter their delegate advantage, while also prioritizing `Gold` and `GreatPeople` for synergy with Venice's strengths and to resolve our GPT deficit.
            
            **Choosing Grand Strategy**
            
            I am now deciding between "United Nations" and "Spaceship" as my Grand Strategy. Given Venice's strengths, "United Nations" seems more beneficial for countering India's diplomatic push and leveraging our trade routes, despite our ongoing Apollo program.
            
            **Selecting Grand Strategy**
            
            I am finalizing my Grand Strategy as "United Nations" to aggressively contest city-states and thwart India's diplomatic victory. This choice prioritizes `Diplomacy`, `Gold`, and `Science`, ensuring we can fund city-state alliances while maintaining strong research capabilities.
            
            **Implementing Diplomatic Measures**
            
            I'm now implementing specific diplomatic measures to counter India's victory. This involves setting my persona to aggressively compete for minor civilizations and bias towards friendliness, alongside enacting the "Free Trade" policy to maximize our gold generation through Great Merchants. My next research target is "Electronics."
            
            **Refining Grand Strategy and Flavors**
            
            I'm now locking in the "United Nations" Grand Strategy. My focus is on maximizing `Diplomacy`, `Gold`, and `GreatPeople` flavors to bolster our city-state influence and counter India's diplomatic push, while maintaining strong `Science` and `Spaceship` for our ongoing progress.
